2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Oracle admission-anchored staging (AAS):** client kv_hints `sglang.next_turn` v1 carry `{session, next_rid, next_gap_s}`. The NextTurnPrefetcher has modes off/observe/on.
     - Case 1 stages the read before arrival under `CacheRequestHandle(next_rid, 0)`.
     - Case 2 re-issues a queued request's own `_prefetch_kvcache`.
   - **Estimators (step 4):**
     - EWMA arrival offset with α=1/8.
     - Admission rate over a 60 s window with 300 s fallback.
     - Waits: W_ql = (k+0.5)/λ and a wait EWMA.
     - L3LoadModel seeds: 0.5 + 0.044 + 0.0025/page + 0.0025/backlog page + 0.36.
     - trigger T = A − L̂.
   - HiCache unified radix cache (URC): host layouts page_first/layer_first, nixl zero-copy, wait_complete policy, #39283 admission re-query, paced retries (8/8), rate_limited.
   - Read-only `resident_prefix_len` probe.
   - Host eviction:
     - Only host-only leaves (evicted from device, backed up, no children, unlocked) are evictable, picked LRU via a heap.
     - Under write-through, the host copies of device-resident nodes can't be host-evicted, so host-only capacity ≈ host − L1 (about 56K tokens in the small-pool smokes).
   - Repo rules: msgspec.Struct not dataclass; comment-style; unit-test-admission (bug regression / derived property / bookkeeping); large-class-style for Scheduler; register_cpu_ci; CustomTestCase.
   - Environment and workflow:
     - Container sglang_hicache, with the repo at /sgl-workspace/sglang.
     - Tests run CPU-only with CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=9.
     - Results dirs are root-owned, so write to them via docker exec.
     - zsh gotchas: no word splitting of $F; `echo ====` fails.

     ```python
     def _wants_refetch(self, plan, *, req, position, wait_s, load_s, now) -> bool:
         cache = self._tree_cache
         # Any read for the request (its own, a re-query or a staged one) joins
         # admission's wait; a second one would fetch the same pages twice.
         if (cache.has_ongoing_prefetch(req.cache_request_handle)
             or now - plan.last_refetch_t < PROBE_INTERVAL_S
             or plan.refetch_capped
             or cache.cache_controller.prefetch_rate_limited()):
             return False
         if plan.refetches > 0:
             # The fetched pages were evicted again before admission.
             plan.refetch_capped = plan.refetches >= MAX_REFETCHES
             _log_event("ntp_reevict", req.rid, evicted=plan.missing_tokens,
                        refetches=plan.refetches, capped=int(plan.refetch_capped))
             if plan.refetch_capped:
                 return False
         plan.last_refetch_t = now
         plan.pending_refetch = RefetchDecision(missing_tokens=plan.missing_tokens,
             position=position, wait_s=wait_s, load_s=load_s)
         return True
     ```
     It is called from `_check_queued`, where `baseline = max(len(plan.key), req.storage_prefetch_last_match_len or 0)` and the probe runs only every 0.5 s (`if now - plan.last_probe_t >= PROBE_INTERVAL_S: plan.missing_tokens = baseline - self._resident_tokens(plan, now=now)`).

     def test_a_landed_read_is_not_taken_for_a_re_eviction(self):
         """A residency probe taken while a re-fetch is in flight goes stale when
         the read lands; acting on it re-fetched pages that were already back

     # The last probe may predate a read that has since landed: look again.

- **Results dirs** are root-owned. Write into them via `docker exec -i sglang_hicache sh -c "cat > ..." < file`.
- **matplotlib** exists only in the container; run plot scripts there.
- **Foreground waits:** `sleep` is blocked, so use until-loops (foreground max ~590–600 s) or `run_in_background`.
- **zsh gotchas on the host:** `$F` is not word-split; `${n^^}` is unsupported.
- **Scratchpad:** `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-wanhr-sglang/7c93b1d5-dc1c-431d-8d3e-efb4ae23a30d/scratchpad`. It is wiped on reboot (memory note). Persistent analysis scripts live in `agent_cache/frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/` (git-ignored).
- **Memory files:** `/home/wanhr/.claude/projects/-home-wanhr-sglang/memory/agent-cache-campaign-7-h200.md` and `MEMORY.md`. Both were updated with c15 results, the c16 launch and the `_wb` finding.
- **Constraint:** "Do not edit agent_cache/scripts/ while it runs" (a campaign). `python/` is safe when a frozen engine is used.

| | wc oracle | to oracle |
|---|---|---|

**Explanations given to the user:**
- Why wc gains were small:
  - FIFO read queue ~9,820 pages (~30 s); 76 % of case-1 reads late.
  - In-flight reads held 39 % of the host pool, so host hits fell 16 → 10 %.
  - The SSD was saturated.
  - Little's law: the backlog cap pins mean TTFT.
- Documented in ORACLE_PLAN.md §5, with options a–e and decision 8.

**`agent_cache/scripts/oracle_timeline.py` written:**

- Uses reconstructed true arrival/admission times (the admit log stamp lags by p50 36 ms).
- Marks reads cut while queued (`ms=0`) with ×.

## Oracle v2 (current task)

The user said: "Now the oracle v2 are implemented in a cpu machine, check agent_cache/ORACLE_V2_PLAN.md and ORACLE_V2_HANDOFF.md for what have been done. Then pick up the unfinished work on this server, implement, test."

New commits from the user: `df900e0d15`, `f1885117c1` (Stage oracle v2 plan and implementation), `c6c8f5fa88` (mutex with polled retries), `5df71c4bf0` (Update data).

**v2 levers:**
- `--hicache-storage-read-order deadline`: reads park without host memory and are dispatched by deadline into a 512-page window; "now" reads bypass the window and budget.
- `--hicache-storage-prefetch-pin-fraction 0.3`: landed spans are host-locked until admission; 600 s leak guard.
- The prefetcher in deadline mode labels deadlines; paced retries are off (poll interval 0) when the oracle and deadline order are both on.
- Online ms/page EWMA.
- `--radix-eviction-policy next_use` (`horizon_s` 300).
- New events: `srs_park`, `srs_dispatch`, `srs_pin`, `srs_pin_skipped`, `srs_unpin`, `srs_rate`, `nue_register`. Summary script `scripts/srs_summary.py`.

**Handoff steps done on the box (2026-10-11):**
- **H1:**
  - The host had rebooted at 18:00Z, and the container was `Exited (255)`. Restarted with `docker start sglang_hicache`.
  - The SSD L3 held 1.6 TB at 70 % from c15. The smoke driver wiped it.
- **H3 (CPU gate in the container):**
  - Eight v2 files passed: 6, 6, 6, 15, 32, 4, 8, 5. The handoff said 5 for flags, but 6 is correct.
  - Unchanged suites passed, including `test_unified_radix_cache_unittest.py` (1,282 passed with the GPU visible) and `test_unified_hicache_regressions.py` (13 with the GPU).
- **H2:** frozen `agent_cache/frozen/engine_5df71c4bf0_v2` (0 diff lines; imports OK).
- **H4 (smokes):**
  - Ran via `frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/v2_smokes.sh`.
  - Deviation: small pools (`L1_TOKENS=65536 HOST_GB=12`, 32×12 at x1 gaps, OFFSET 32), because a NAT160 32-session smoke never fills the host pool.
  - Results in `results/oracle_v2_smokes_1011_1831/` and `_1848/`. Every arm: all boot checks OK, 384 turns, 0 errors, 0 tracebacks.

| arm | parks (now) | dispatches; launched=0 | pins / unpins | other |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_dl` | 176 (176) | 176; 33 | 97 / 97 (admit) | — |
| `_oracle_v2` | 504 (27) | 504; 136 | 133 / 133 | 99 % of triggers fired ahead (lead > load); timed deadlines p50 23.5 s ahead; window held (in-flight p99 477) |
| `_oracle_v2_nu` | 509 | 509; 113 | 136 / 136 | 736 `nue_register` |
| `_wb` | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 L3 hits of 1,281 lookups |

- `launched=0` is a small-pool artifact: L1 mirror 65,536 + prefetch cap 61,056 > 122,112-token host. At NAT160 campaign 16's `_dl` showed 0.

**Problems found and fixed:**
1. **Zombie servers.**
   - After the reboot, the container's PID 1 (`/bin/zsh`, no init) does not reap. Exited servers stay zombies, `kill -0` reports them alive, `stop_server.sh` waits 120 s, and `start_server.sh` refuses the next boot.
   - Fix: an `alive()` helper (checks `ps -o stat=` is not Z) in `stop_server.sh`, `start_server.sh` (double-boot guard and boot-wait) and `start_client.sh`. Uncommitted.
2. **Handoff H5 command bug.** It used `$(docker exec ...)` inside the container's `bash -c`, so `ENGINE_PYTHONPATH` would have been empty. Launched with an in-container `ls` instead.
3. **`_wb` held.**
   - Diagnosis, verified: `Scheduler._prefetch_kvcache` only issues an L3 lookup when `last_host_node` (= `best_match_node`, the deepest device-or-host match) is host-backed or the root. Under write_back that node is usually device-only, so the lookup and the #39283 re-query are skipped. Of 133 recomputed turns, 70 issued no lookup and 63 only tail lookups (largest p50 576 tokens).
   - Origin: upstream code. The gate dates back to the original storage layer, whose comment read "only to initiate the prefetch if the last node is backuped; otherwise, the allocated GPU memory must be locked for integrity". It was refactored by `cd145f840f` (2026-07-25); PR #34798 (`977412ae617`, 2026-08-18) added buffer-mode re-anchoring.
   - Upstream PR #31902 (`4ea17169b0`, 2026-07-27) made the tree accept write-back refills under un-backed parents, but the scheduler gate was never updated.
   - Upstream validation accepts write_back + storage in `cache` mode; it only rejects it with `buffer_only`.
   - Device nodes do carry page hashes when storage is on (`unified_tree_core.py` line ~1485).
   - Told the user: it is not write_back's design per se but an unfinished upstream combination of the storage-layer integrity choice and write_back. A fix would need locking the device-only anchor during the read (or re-anchoring as buffer mode does).

## In progress when context ended

The user asked: "Based on what we've discussed, implemented and evaluated so far. Is there some critical issue that we've missed or my questions haven't touched yet?" I was gathering evidence. Findings so far, to present (text answer pending):

1. **KV correctness is never validated.**
   - `--check-ids` only verifies that previous-reply token ids are reused in the next prompt; campaigns run with CHECK_IDS=0.
   - The model's (possibly garbage) replies are appended to history, so a wrong L3 restore (v1/v2 trimming, partial allocation, pins) would go unnoticed.
   - Suggest a logprob/KL check comparing restored vs fresh prefill (the `kl-consistency-test` skill).
2. **The L3 cleaner deletes cached data mid-run, unattributed.**
   - Each 5-h arm writes ~3.2 TB (c15 wc_oracle h2s 32.9M tokens = 3.23 TB) to a 2.3 TB SSD.
   - "NIXL L3 cleanup: deleted ~39–40K groups / ~79K files (~230 GiB)" at 70 % → 60 %, twice per arm in c14 wc (00:10:23, 00:56:00), c15 wc_oracle (19:52:37, 20:42:15), c15 to_oracle (02:09:08) and c14 to (06:37:45).
   - The cleaner deletes oldest-mtime groups. Misses caused by it are counted as recompute; neither the oracle nor next-use governs L3 retention.
   - Campaign 16's `_dl` L3 was already 655 GB (29 %) after ~50 min.
3. **Oracle hints are exact** (trace gaps; arrival error ~45–123 ms), so all gains are upper bounds. No misprediction sensitivity study was done (v1 E4 "gap × 0.75 / 1.25, noise" never run); next_use could be worse than LRU with noisy hints. Hints are also a trust/abuse surface in multi-tenant serving (a client can claim "soon" to protect its contexts).
4. **Statistics:**
   - Single runs and cross-campaign baselines; bistable/limit-cycle controller dynamics.
   - Turn-level bootstrap ignores within-conversation correlation; a cluster bootstrap is needed.
   - The "congested bins" metric was defined post hoc; the plan's second pass ("TTFT at a fixed user cap with two seeds") is not scheduled.
5. **Generality:** one MoE model with small KV per token (96 KiB), one GPU/SSD, x70 gap scaling, TP=1 only.
6. **Unmeasured metrics:** TPOT/decode effects, per-user fairness (next-use and deadlines may starve long-gap users), session/task completion time.
7. **Other:** the write_back + L3 upstream gap could be reported upstream; the container should ideally be recreated with `--init` (zombie issue); script fixes and doc updates are uncommitted.

2. Key Technical Concepts

   **System under test**
   - SGLang HiCache three tiers: L1 GPU (668,160 tokens), L2 host 160 GB (1,627,648 tokens), L3 nixl SSD at `/mnt/ssd` (2.3 TB, cleaner 70/60).
   - Policies: write-through; `wait_complete` vs `timeout`.
   - Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507, bf16 KV at 96 KiB/token; 64-token pages of 6 MiB.
   - Oracle v1: next-turn prefetch, case-1 staging, case-2 refetch.
   - Oracle v2 levers:
     - deadline read order with a 512-page window;
     - pins (pin fraction 0.3);
     - next-turn prefetch on;
     - paced retries off (poll interval 0);
     - `next_use` eviction (horizon 300 s).
   - Arms: `three_tier_wc`, `_dl`, `_oracle_v2`, `_oracle_v2_nu`.
   - Paced retries / lookup budget: 8 attempts at poll interval 8, shared with the upstream #39283 admission re-query.

   **Failure modes**
   - Lost after landing (x70 storms): fixed by pins.
   - Retry-budget exhaustion at x10 (the campaign 11 failure): a new tail of ≥256 tokens is never stored, so paced polls spend the budget, history is LRU-evicted while queued, and the re-query is refused.
   - Hidden side effect: polls refresh LRU recency via `match_prefix`.

   **Evaluation method**
   - Finite workload: 768 users. Compare on shared turns, time until N users finished, and admission waits (not part of TTFT).
   - Little's law holds; the backlog-driven cap (old client).
   - Statistics: bootstrap resampling users vs resampling time blocks.

   **New replay client (REPLAY_REDESIGN.md, commits `e32458cc9f` + `593da72038`)**
   - Knobs: `TRACE USERS TURNS ARRIVAL GAP ORACLE_HINTS C SEED CLIENT_TIMEOUT`.
   - Fixed concurrency cap `C`: idle users hold their slots; waits are outside TTFT.
   - No stationary start, no 30–50 turn draws, no backlog cap.
   - Gap cap fixed at 1,200 s.
   - The retired-knob guard refuses `run_campaign14.sh`.
   - New runs do not pair with campaigns 13–17.
   - Records: `run`, `conv_start`, `turn`, `conv_end`, `conv_skipped`, `users` (every 60 s), `summary`.
   - `run_compare.sh` does the per-arm L3 wipe, boot and client, and writes the `compare_<UTC>` manifest.

   **Operations**
   - Frozen engine `agent_cache/frozen/engine_5df71c4bf0_v2/python` (identical to the current `python/`).
   - The container's PID 1 doesn't reap; scripts treat zombie pids as stopped.
   - The host reboots frequently; restart the container manually.
   - Use `docker exec -i` for heredocs.
   - Bash reads scripts incrementally, so don't edit running scripts.

   #!/usr/bin/env bash
   # Campaigns 18 (x70) and 19 (x10) on the redesigned client ... resumable by arm ...
   # Stop: pkill -f "[c]18_c19.sh"; pkill -f "^bash [r]un_compare.sh"; pkill -f "[r]eplay_agentic.py"; bash stop_server.sh

   AC=/sgl-workspace/sglang/agent_cache

   ENGINE=$(ls -d "$AC/frozen/engine_5df71c4bf0_v2/python") || { echo "STOP: frozen engine missing"; exit 1; }
   ALL_ARMS="three_tier_wc three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu"
   missing() { local A M=""; for A in $ALL_ARMS; do grep -q "^$A " "$1/manifest.txt" || M="$M $A"; done; echo "${M# }"; }
   run_one() {   # <campaign dir> <gap scale>
     ... TODO=$(missing "$CD"); LOG="$CD/driver_$(date -u +%m%d_%H%M%S).log"
     nohup iostat -dxt vdc 10 >> "$CD/iostat_vdc.log" 2>&1 < /dev/null & IOS=$!
     echo "LAUNCH ... arms: $TODO gaps x$G commit ... engine $ENGINE -> $LOG"
     ARMS="$TODO" LEVEL=NAT160 MODEL=Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507 CTX=131072 KV_DTYPE=auto TEMPLATE= L3_CLEANER_PCT=70,60 \
       TRACE="$AC/traces/lmcache_agentic_trace_262k.json" USERS=768 TURNS=50 ARRIVAL=0.1 GAP="$G" C=128 SEED=0 \

     M=$(missing "$CD"); [ -z "$M" ] || { echo "STOP: ... missing: $M ..."; return 1; }
   }
   run_one "$AC/results/campaign18" 70 && run_one "$AC/results/campaign19" 10 && echo "CAMPAIGNS DONE $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
   ```

4. Errors and fixes

   - **Heredoc not reaching the container:** `docker exec` without `-i` dropped the heredoc stdin, so the Python zip script did nothing. Fixed by using `docker exec -i`.
   - **`zip` not installed** (host or container): used Python `zipfile` with `ZIP_DEFLATED`, `compresslevel=9`. Verified with `unzip -t` and `cmp`.
   - **`c15_diagnosis.py` path doubling:** it joins `--results` with the run name, so passing `results/<dir>` gave `results/results/...`. Fixed by passing bare names.
   - **Workflow output not parseable:** the JSON parse of the output file failed, so I used `journal.jsonl` and mapped `agentId` → label from the "started" entries.
   - **Grey identical colors in `steady.png`:** wrote the new `c16_figure.py` with distinct colors. The legend then overlapped the title; fixed with `fig.suptitle` + `fig.legend` + `tight_layout(rect=...)`.
   - **zsh "no matches found"** on globs such as `$D/campaign19/driver_*.log` before the file exists: the command exited 1 in the last status check. Work around it by avoiding unmatched globs or splitting the commands.
   - **`REPLAY_REDESIGN.md` missing on the box:** `git fetch origin main` found commit `593da72038` "redesign doc"; I fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only origin/main` (the tree was clean).
   - **Previous-session c17 doc review workflow** (`wf_0c0b49c1-dc1`) was stopped by the session end. Not resumed: ultracode is now off, so Workflow needs user opt-in.

2. Key Technical Concepts

   **System**
   - SGLang HiCache three tiers:
     - L1 GPU, 668,160 tokens;
     - L2 host, 160 GB = 1,627,648 tokens;
     - L3 nixl SSD at `/mnt/ssd`, 2.3 TB, cleaner 70→60 %.
   - Write-through, `wait_complete`.
   - `max_running_requests=64`; in practice the running batch is bounded by the KV pool (~30–35 requests).
   - Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507, 96 KiB/token.

   **Arms**
   - `three_tier_wc`: the original HiCache, with paced retries of 8 attempts at poll interval 8 and the #39283 admission re-query.
   - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu`: pins at 0.3 (488K-token budget), deadline order with a 512-page window, oracle next-turn prefetch, paced retries off, next-use eviction (horizon 300 s, overdue grace 60 s).

   **Next-use eviction tiers:** ended 0, overdue 1 (next use more than 60 s past), far 2, unknown 3, soon 4. A queued session is registered at arrival (`nue_register next_use_ms=0`), so after 60 s queued it becomes overdue.

   **Redesigned client**
   - `USERS=768 TURNS=50 ARRIVAL=0.1 GAP=70|10 C=128 SEED=0 ORACLE_HINTS=1 CLIENT_TIMEOUT=18000`; 24,879 turns.
   - Fixed C; idle users hold their slots.
   - At timeout: no new sends, in-flight requests complete; `capped` conv_end records; `conv_skipped` for users who never started.
   - No mid-session start, no 30–50 turn draw.
   - Records: `users` (cap, live, waiting, in_flight, admitted, finished), `conv_start` (queued_s, start_s, arrival_s), `turn`, `conv_end` (`capped`), `summary`.

   **Storm mechanism (c17, repeated in c19):**
   1. A new tail of ≥ 256 tokens makes the request poll the SSD for it.
   2. The polls spend the lookup budget ("reissue cap reached").
   3. History is evicted while the request is queued.
   4. The #39283 re-query is refused, so the history is recomputed.
   5. Recompute displaces decode and the queue grows, so more turns lose history.
   - The old client's backlog cap shed load (128 → ~90/76) and ended c17's storm. The fixed C=128 never sheds load, so c19's storm lasted until the stop.

   **Statistics:** paired TTFT on shared turns; bootstrap resampling users and resampling 10-min time blocks.

## 3. Key verified findings (for the final discussion reply)

**What the oracle hint carries.** It bundles three signals:
1. **Session linkage** (`next_rid`). `session_id` reaches `Req` but no hint consumer reads it, and the replay client does not send it.
2. **End of session.** The last turn carries no hint and is registered ENDED. A realistic source is a parsed finish call or `/close_session`.
3. **Gap value.** Not realistic: the hint for turn k+1 rides on turn k's request, before reply k (the tool call that sets the gap) exists.

**Engine facts**
- Only `NextTurnPrefetcher.on_turn_committed` reads hints (`python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_prefetch.py:245-309`).
- A missing, malformed or unknown-version hint means ENDED (`:265-277`, `:96-101`).
- Plans are keyed by client `next_rid`.
- Constants: expiry = predicted + max(60 s, gap) (`:297`); overdue grace 60 s; FAR horizon 300 s; lead horizon 120 s.
- No trust boundary: `Infinity` passes, and `io_struct.py:362-367`'s "trusted orchestrator" is only a comment.
- `_oracle_v2` with `ORACLE_HINTS=0` ≈ `_dl` + poll 0 (inferred). This is a no-code hint-free comparator, but the c18/c19 launchers set `ORACLE_HINTS=1`, so it must be overridden.
- `--hicache-storage-prefetch-retry-poll-interval 0` works in any configuration.
- The tool-call parser runs HTTP-side (`serving_chat.py:2546` non-streaming, `:2974` streaming) and only with `tools` plus `--tool-call-parser`.
- The replay's replies are synthetic, so testing server-side parsing needs a reply-class shim.

**Trace predictability**
- Tool name explains 73.6 % of ln-gap variance.
- Adding the command: R² 0.805, and AUC for gap > 4.29 s real (300 s at x70) rises from 0.70 to 0.90. Best model R² 0.832.
- An online per-(source, tool, class) table learned from earlier sessions: R² 0.766 (0.776 after 100 sessions), AUC 0.845.
- Per-session history alone is weak: R² ≤ 0.42.
- Realistic errors are one-sided on long gaps. For x70 gaps > 300 s the median truth/prediction ratio is 2.94, and 41 % are more than 4× too short.
- Under v2 rules the realistic S5 hint expires 60 % of > 300 s gaps into ENDED.
- Ranking idle sessions by per-class survival: AUC 0.900 (x70) / 0.952 (x10), against LRU 0.763 / 0.922. v2 order with a point hint: 0.825 / 0.837, losing to LRU at x10.
- LRU + an ended flag: AUC 0.990 at x10.
- Recording artifacts:
  - a 300-s harness stall: 73 gaps, 0.30 % of gaps, 80.5 % of x10-far KV-token-seconds;
  - the 1,200-s cap;
  - the 50-call cap: 228 of 768 sessions end there.
- 47 % of x70 returns fall at 45–60 s (a 0.7-s polling grid × 70).

**Calibrated cache sim** (x70, 959K host-only room)
- SSD-served returns: LRU 22.0 %, oracle 7.7 %.
- Share of the oracle's gain kept:

| information | kept |
|---|---|
| idle age only (P6) | 27–32 % |
| tool + age (P7) | 32–38 % |
| P7 + exact end (P9) | 48–54 % |
| tool-median hint (P7m) | 50–54 % |
| noise σ = 0.5 | 86–88 % |
| end only | 32–34 % (1–7 % at 318–578K rooms) |
| end + hazard | 45–69 % |

- Missing hint treated as ENDED at 50 % dropout: −49 to −64 % (worse than LRU).
- At x10, hint-free queue protection (P0a) keeps 78–94 % of the gap.
- A 5-s ping hides 85–100 % of SSD reads at SSD utilisation ≤ 0.56, and ≤ 2 % when saturated.
- LRU with 291K more host-room tokens (~28 GB) beats every realistic signal. Mirror reclaim or write-back is the rival.

**Evidence**
- The hint-driven share of the gains is unmeasured:
  - c16 next-use −5.92 s was on the old client, where `_nu` escaped the backlog;
  - c15 v1 −1.41 s / −7.28 s were across campaigns;
  - c18 −0.51 s is undetermined, since run-to-run spread at ~1 s TTFT is unmeasured;
  - x10 was the baseline storm.
- At real cadence (median gap 0.71 s) the host/SSD tiers are never reached on this box. In campaign 10, SSD restores were 2.6 % of returns, all after 7.7–33 min pauses: −2.20 s after ≥ 10-min gaps, −0.14 s overall.

**Literature**
- Production APIs sell retention TTLs (Anthropic 5 min / 1 h, OpenAI 30m / 12h, Gemini explicit TTL), never next-turn times.
- Duration fields exist only as RFCs (SGLang #24656, vLLM #57103). Tokencake takes developer `predict_time`.
- Prior art: Continuum (per-tool TTL), CacheWise (per-tool + elapsed, GPU), CachedAttention (queue look-ahead), PEEK, InferCept (93 % of oracle), T-LRU (in the engine as `tlru`), the learning-augmented caching theory (Lykouris–Vassilvitskii: Belady on predictions is unbounded).
- TraceLab v0.0.2 has 8,058 real Claude Code/Codex sessions. Per its paper, human think time dominates wall-clock (median wait 1.4 min, p90 20.6 min).

**Recommended position** (`design/final_position.md`)
- Option 2 (engine-inferred) as the default.
- Option 1 as an optional checked prior, through a v2 schema with explicit `ended`, and only after the engine change ships.
- Option 3 categorical facts, ranked: queue/arrival > session end > waiting-for-human > ping > tool class / timeout > retention class.
- Architecture:
  - one `ReturnForecast` per idle session: survival curve plus an ended mass, from censored per-command Kaplan-Meier tables with backoff;
  - eviction tiers ENDED → FAR → UNKNOWN (LRU) → SOON → QUEUED;
  - a ghost-LRU fallback and a shadow mode;
  - exact hints become a degenerate forecast, so the oracle stays the upper bound on the same code path.
- Hint-free fixes regardless of hints:
  - missing hint = UNKNOWN;
  - queued sessions QUEUED until admission, never overdue (open issue 6; `--schedule-policy lpm` is a no-code candidate);
  - expiry never ends a session;
  - plan-free refetch and deadlines for every queued request;
  - poll 0, keeping #39283;
  - pins only for arrived requests; staging off by default;
  - skip the arrival SSD lookup for fresh tails;
  - nixl write dedup.
- Measurement ladder:
  1. CPU sim ladder, ~1 CPU-day.
  2. Restored-KV correctness check, 1–2 GPU-h.
  3. x10 hint-free comparator plus a replicate plus an LPM arm, ~15 GPU-h.
  4. x70 eviction-only exact-hint arm (observe mode + next_use, grace 1e9, poll 0, no staging) plus the floor plus a same-seed c18 replicate, ~15 GPU-h.
  5. TraceLab audit.
  6. Load sweep with the fixed floor.
  7. End-only arm.
  8. The forecaster with a reply shim.
  9. Ping.
  
  About 75 GPU-h at one load point, ~115 with the sweep.

Right now, we are running the trace driven evaluations, to make it practicle like the real system, we should update the hint's accuracy to mimic the real system and workload. In this direction, what do you think? The agentic system (like claude code/ other agent system) should provide the kv hint since it should know its own workload, right?"

## 3. Key facts gathered (for the answer)

**Harness (Claude Code)**
- It knows the kind of wait exactly:
  - tool, via `PreToolUse` (tool input includes timeout and `run_in_background`);
  - an approval pending (`PermissionRequest`);
  - waiting for the user, or for background work and crons (`Stop` arrays);
  - compaction (`PreCompact`/`PostCompact`; header `x-claude-code-context-compacted` means "prefix no longer used, cache can be dropped");
  - session end (`SessionEnd`);
  - measured durations (`PostToolUse` `duration_ms`; header `x-claude-code-prev-tool-durations`).
- Gateway hint headers (v2.1.273+) are sent by default to the Anthropic API; on a custom base URL they need `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS=1`. They carry request class, agent type, compaction, prev tool durations, prompt id, and session/agent ids. These are facts, not forecasts.
- Cache TTL is 1 h for the main conversation on a subscription and 5 min otherwise. It is chosen by request type and plan, not by a predicted gap.
- Bash: default timeout 120 s, max 600 s (confirmed).
- ScheduleWakeup's "1 min–1 h" range is documented; the clamping is not.
- Typing is visible only through a mod.

**Other harnesses**
- Codex sends only `prompt_cache_key`.
- OpenHands V0 polls every 0.5 s and returns after 10 s without output; the default cap is 500 iterations (the trace used 50).
  - 94.8 % of SWE-bench bash gaps between 0.6 and 10 s sit on the 0.70 + 0.5k s grid.
  - 54 of 207 gaps ≥ 10 s fall in [10, 11) s.
  - The 300-s spike is unexplained.
- What the server can never infer: approvals, user presence or typing, background tasks and crons, client back-off, abandonment vs exit, upcoming compaction, caps.

**TraceLab**
- The HF release is v0.0.2 (2026-07-24): 8,058 sessions (Claude Code 5,319, Codex 2,739), 665,453 model calls, 743,819 tool calls, 2.69M timing events, 52 users, 77.2 MB, CC BY 4.0.
- The paper (arXiv 2606.30560) covers v0.0.1: 4,265 sessions.
- Events: tool_call, tool_result, reasoning, usage_report, text, user_message. No approvals, compaction, start/end, subagent links, typing, or tool arguments.
- Human waits: median 1.4 min, p90 20.6 min, p99 13.9 h (Table 7; counts any positive gap before a user message, including non-human ones). The v0.0.2 docs give p90 15.2 min.
- Human waiting is 92.3 % of wall-clock (64.3 % with gaps capped at 1 h).
- Tool calls over 1 min are 4 % of calls but 85 % of tool time. Compaction appears in 9.7 % of sessions.
- Feasibility:
  - It can calibrate tool, timer, AskUserQuestion and human end-of-turn waits, but not permission waits or typing.
  - A human-paced replay needs our own converter: about 1 CPU-day of analysis plus 0.5–1 day for the converter.
  - Caveats: resumed-session duplicates; contexts exceed 262k tokens (p90 467k); there are too few users to load a server, so sessions must be overlaid.

**Replay and engine**
- The hint rides on turn k's request; rids look like `<tag>-c<user>-t<turn>`; the gap is the recorded gap × scale, capped at 1,200 s.
- No hint on the last turn; the hint is suppressed when it would exceed the timeout. `--turns 50` cuts nothing.
- The engine reads the hint at commit (`next_turn_prefetch.py:265`, called from `unified_radix_cache.py:1168-1176`). c18/c19 had 0 expiries.
- `realistic_hints.csv` joins on 24,879/24,879 rows.
  - `pred_s5_s` is identical to `qgbt_q50_s`.
  - Quantiles cross on 1,598 rows.
  - The spike is never foreseen.
- Missing, malformed or v2-only payloads are treated as ENDED, so today "no hints" equals "end only".
- No standalone hint channel exists: the dispatcher at `scheduler.py:1730-1836` has none, and `/close_session` never reaches the prefetcher.
- Engine changes needed, roughly 110–160 lines plus tests:
  - expiry never ends a session;
  - missing = unknown, plus an explicit `ended`;
  - queued turns never overdue;
  - a quantile payload.
- Piggybacking the hint emulates a post-reply message: the analogous real delay is 82/94 ms at p50 and ~1 s at p99. It cannot emulate updates during the wait.

**Critic and fact-check**
- Better framing: "Clients declare, engines forecast."
- Objections raised:
  - "superset" is false: the engine knows its queue and the pooled statistics;
  - the ladder ranks information, not producers;
  - the out-of-fold hints aren't causal;
  - fix the engine first;
  - resolve the ladder in the sim and run ≥ 3 GPU replicates;
  - a human-paced workload should be primary;
  - add a host-room rival, partial adoption and an over-claimer;
  - the novelty claim is overclaimed.
- KVFlow sends an order, not a time. Continuum is serving-side.
- The sim models no plan expiry. The TraceLab numbers come from the paper's Table 7, not lit A24.

**Numbers from `critic_numbers/`**
- R² of ln-gap by learner:

| Learner | R² |
|---|---|
| Own session only | 0.42 |
| Per repo | 0.70–0.74 |
| Fleet table O4 | 0.76–0.77 |
| Repo with fleet backoff | 0.77–0.78 |

- AUC above 30 s real: 0.831 for the out-of-fold model vs 0.70–0.72 for causal learners.
- x70 gaps over 300 s:

| Hint | Median truth/hint | > 4× too short | Expire under today's rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5 (out-of-fold) | 2.94 | 41 % | 60.3 % |
| O4 (online fleet table) | 3.93 | 49 % | 87.2 % |
| O3 (own session) | 7.56 | 69 % | 83.1 % |
| qGBT p90 | — | — | 28 % |

- At x10, gaps over 300 s expire 93–100 % of the time.
- Staging for x70 gaps over 300 s: S5 wastes 61 % of staged reads and holds 1.79× the exact hints' token-seconds; O4 wastes 87 % at 1.75×.

**Sim numbers**
- σ 0.5 keeps 86–88 %; P7m keeps 50–54 % (x70, 959K room).
- 47 % of x70 returns fall at 45–60 s.
- +291K tokens (~28 GB) of host room beats every realistic signal.
- The c18 oracle effect was −0.51 s TTFT; run-to-run spread is unmeasured.
- Exact gaps still left admission error p10..p90 of −7..+8 s at x10.

## 4. Pending: send the final answer (drafted, matching `position_v2.md`)

**Content of the reply**
- **Short answer.** Mostly yes: the agent should be the hint's source. What it knows exactly is the kind of wait, not when it returns. So it should send facts plus, optionally, its own estimate, and the engine forecasts ("agent declares, engine forecasts"). Claude Code already does this.
- **Revision.** This changes my last answer, which had the engine inferring everything by default.
- **Table** of agent-known signals and the Claude Code mechanisms that expose them.
- **Where the premise stops:**
  - Durations come from history: R² 0.42 / 0.70–0.74 / 0.76–0.77 / ~0.78.
  - Errors are one-sided: 3.9× too short, 49 % > 4×.
  - Human waits run from 1 min to 13 h.
  - The engine knows its queue.
  - "System status" splits the same way: the agent's own status is exact; the server's status only the engine sees.
- **Making the evaluation realistic:**
  1. Model the agent's knowledge, not noise. Learn online in the client. Piggybacking is OK (~0.1 s), but there are no in-gap updates.
  2. Fix the engine first, using the numbers above.
  3. Add a TraceLab human-paced workload (77 MB).
  4. Compare on one fixed engine: floor / + facts / + agent estimates / + ping / oracle, with a host-room rival, partial adoption and an over-claimer. Run the CPU sim first; on GPU, only decisive pairs with ≥ 3 repeats.
- **Demo path later:** `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` → `/v1/messages`. Gaps: the adapter drops hints and session ids, and there is no post-reply channel.
- **Questions for the user:**
  1. Accept "agent declares, engine forecasts", with agent estimates as one arm?
  2. Add the TraceLab human-paced workload (77 MB download)?
  3. Start with the CPU sim, then the engine fixes, then GPU pairs?
- **Housekeeping:** no engine/script/result changes and no GPU runs; reports are in `agent_cache/frozen/hint_realism_20261027/design/client_hint/`.

Do not start any experiment, engine change, download or commit until the user decides.

## 2. Findings this segment (recorded in docs)

**c18 +17 % SSD reads, ORACLE_FINDINGS §9.10.** Scripts in `agent_cache/frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c18_ssd_reads/`: c18_reads.py, exposure.py, ttft_cost.py, outputs, README.
- Of 5,520 staged reads, 1,558 were left unpinned because the pin budget (488K tokens) was full.
- 1,553 of those (99.7 %) were evicted before arrival and re-read: 3.78 TB, 27 % of the arm's reads. Without the re-reads the arm would read 10.2 TB vs the baseline's 12.0 TB (−15 %). Pinned staged spans were never re-read (0/3,962).
- Cause, in code:
  - A host-leaf eviction deletes the node (`unified_tree_core.py` `_evict_host_leaf` → `_remove_leaf_from_parent`). Ids come from a counter (`UnifiedTreeNode.id`).
  - Staged and re-fetched spans are re-inserted as new nodes that are never registered. `_on_arrival` re-registers the old, deleted tail id. So an unpinned span ranks UNKNOWN, below every SOON session.
- At light load, staged reads land a median 77 s early, which overflows the pin budget.
- LRU control (c16 `_oracle_v2`): only 19 % re-read at 10–30 s exposure.
- c16 `_nu`: 643 turns re-read, 1.53 TB. c19: 66 turns, 0.18 TB.
- Re-read turns' TTFT: 2.43 s (oracle) vs 2.48 s (baseline). Staged-once turns: 0.55 s vs 2.13 s.

**c19 60-s waits, ORACLE_FINDINGS §9.11.** Scripts in `frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c19_cleanup_waits/`: c19_waits.py, timeline5s.py, trace_turns.py, evict_timing.py, cleanup_hits.py, capped_refetch.py, outputs, README.
- The overdue rank caused only 4 of the 695 queued losses (4 of 26 among the 67 long waits).
- The cleanups didn't slow the SSD (3.0–3.2 ms/page; SSD 94–99 % busy throughout).
- In the 5 min after the second cleanup (client 10,031 s), 7 turns hit the re-fetch cap and recomputed 166K tokens: 6 whole histories (15–52K each) and 1 partial (3.5K).
- Two causes:
  - unpinned re-fetched spans evicted within milliseconds (2 in the window, 5 in the run);
  - the cleaner deleted shared prefix pages. A page that is only read keeps its first-write mtime, and lookups match prefixes, so whole histories became unreachable (`storage_hit=0`). 4 turns after the second cleanup, 2 after the first.
- At x10, 8,521 turns (35 %) had their whole history evicted a median 2.1 s after commit, then staged back. Two reasons:
  - next-use ranks the farthest SOON session first, and a just-finished session's predicted admission includes ~45 s of queue wait;
  - `drive_host_eviction` (`components/full.py`) re-pushes the parent with an unregistered (UNKNOWN) rank, so the eviction cascades up the whole session.

**ORACLE_FINDINGS.md edits (uncommitted)**
- Header line; two §1 summary bullets.
- §9.6 pointer; §9.7 sentence; new §9.10 and §9.11.
- §10 caveat.
- §11: intro note "Issues 12 and 13 and the additions to 6 and 9 … On 2026-10-29 the user decided to record them now and fix them later."
- Additions to issues 5, 6, 8 and 9; new issues 12 (register staged/re-fetched spans; just-in-time staging; park when the pin budget is full) and 13 (whole-session eviction design question).
- §12 rows and rerun commands for both analyses.
- Glossary: "next-use registry", "re-fetch cap".
- §9.11 was corrected: 6 whole histories + 1 partial; the c19 README was corrected to match.

Memory updated: `agent-cache-campaign-7-h200.md` (c18 and c19 explanations) and `hint-realism-analysis.md`. MEMORY.md line updated.

## 3. Latest task: implementation done (uncommitted)

**Trace check (premise confirmed)**
- `traces/lmcache_agentic_trace_262k.json` (768 conversations, 24,879 turns) has no tool field. Turn k+1's first message header names the tool reply k called: "EXECUTION RESULT of [x]", "[TOOL RESULT x]", error/nudge texts.
- Every one of the 24,111 returning turns gets a label.
- swebench: execute_bash 68.5 %, str_replace_editor 18.9 %, ERR:execute_bash 6.7 %.
- gaia: web_browser_go 46.6 %, bash 23.2 %, python 16 %.
- wildclaw: exec 85 %.

**New `agent_cache/scripts/gap_hints.py` (stdlib)**
- `tool_category(turn)`.
- `GapEstimate` NamedTuple (family, mu, sigma, n, basis), with quantile, mean, cdf, log_density, point, payload.
- `GapHistory(family="lognormal"|"normal", scope="category"|"session", min_samples=2, sigma_floor=0.5)`. Fallback order: category → session → None. GAP_FLOOR_S 0.01.
- `TurnHint`; `SessionHinter(turns, gap_of, history, categories=True)` with `step(t)`, order-enforced: observe the gap before turn t, then estimate the gap after it.
- `next_turn_envelope`: v2 action {session, next_rid, category, gap: {family, mu, sigma, n, basis, p10, p50, p90} or null}, then an optional v1 action {session, next_rid, next_gap_s = point}.
- `ended_envelope` (v2 {session, ended: true}); `hint_record`.

**`replay_agentic.py`**
- New `--hints none|oracle|dist`; `--oracle-hints` is kept as an alias (resolves to `args.oracle_hints`).
- Dist settings: `--hint-family`, `--hint-scope`, `--hint-min-samples`, `--hint-sigma-floor` (0.5), `--hint-point p50|mean|none` (default p50), `--hint-end explicit|none`.
- `new_hinter()`, `dist_hint()`; `play()` uses `dist_hint` when a hinter exists. Records `dist_hint`.
- No timeout suppression in dist mode. The oracle path is byte-identical.

**Launchers**
- `start_client.sh`: HINTS knob (derived from ORACLE_HINTS when unset) plus HINT_FAMILY, HINT_SCOPE, HINT_MIN_N, HINT_SIGMA_FLOOR, HINT_POINT, HINT_END, with STOP checks. Verified: ORACLE_HINTS=1 gives exactly `--oracle-hints`.
- `run_compare.sh`: exports the set knobs and appends `HINT_CFG` to config.txt (delay_components.py only parses `arms=`).

**`hint_eval.py`**
- Offline evaluator. Report: `agent_cache/results/analysis_dist_hints/hint_eval.txt`.
- Results at x70, default settings:
  - 6.4 % of gaps get no estimate; 86.4 % use the category basis.
  - Share below p10/p50/p90: 7.1 / 55.7 / 91.0 %.
  - <60 s: median true/p50 0.92. 60–300 s: 24.5 % above p90.
  - ≥300 s (918 gaps): 84 % above p90, median true/p50 6.89; today's engine would expire 92 %.
  - Scope "session": worse (14.85×).
- Raw-Gaussian ("normal") log scores are catastrophic (−104 to −60,000) vs lognormal (−4.6 on the common subset). Floor 0.5 ≈ 0.75 ≫ 0.25/0.1; min_samples 2 is best.
- x10, ≥300 s: 94 % above p90, median 79×.

**Tests**
- `test_gap_hints.py`: 23 tests, all pass in the venv `agent_cache/frozen/hint_realism_20261027/venv/bin/python` (msgspec 0.22.0). On host python3, 22 pass and 1 is skipped.
- Uses a stub aiohttp. The host lacks aiohttp and msgspec; run as `cd agent_cache/scripts && python3 -m unittest test_gap_hints`. `-I` breaks the sibling imports.
- The container `sglang_hicache` has been exited for 10 days (not started). GPU idle.

## 2. Immediate open task: answer "what's the first, the 50-call cap?"

Decision 1 in `agent_cache/DIST_HINT_DESIGN.md` §12: read the cap as the harness's (`--hint-end explicit`) in GPU arms, and as the dataset's window (`natural`) only in rule replay and simulation? Recommendation yes: explicit ends sessions where oracle arms end them, so A3 vs A4 isolates the gap value (up to ~20 turns the oracle leaves unhinted before its timeout; 21 in c18).

**Evidence just gathered (not yet reported to the user):**
- 228 of 768 sessions have exactly 50 turns (n_rows = n_turns = 50); all SWE-bench: minimax-m2.5 189 of 490, deepseek-v3.1 29 of 65, claude-sonnet-4-6 10 of 110. GAIA max 28, WildClaw max 29. Spike: length 49 has 16 sessions, 50 has 228. 29.7 % of all session ends. 226 of 228 start at turn 0; midrun_start: 136 SWE-bench + 1 WildClaw overall.
- **The HF dataset card largely resolves it** (huggingface.co/datasets/sammshen/lmcache-agentic-traces, via WebFetch; summarized by a small model, quotes): SWE-bench sessions are "the longest (median 38 turns, many hitting the 50-turn cap)"; "Sessions range from 5-50 turns of edit-test-debug cycles"; sessions run "until done or the turn budget is exhausted". It does not state `max_iterations` nor give the budget's value for GAIA/WildClaw, and doesn't say sessions are truncated/windowed; `input[N]` is a strict prefix-superset of `input[N-1]`; `pre_gap` "Always 0.0 for the first iteration of a session", "Seconds between the previous iteration's response completing (last streamed token) and this iteration's request being sent", "real tool-execution / user-thinking time"; median 0.71 s, mean 2.08 s, p95 3.73 s. This favours the **harness budget reading** ("turn budget") over a dataset window; REALISTIC_HINTS §9 and critic.md had called it unresolved ("fits a harness iteration cap or a first-50-rows dataset window equally well"; `frozen/hint_realism_20261027/design/client_hint/critic_numbers/cap_midrun.out`). Final replies aren't in the data (no output column).
- What the decision changes: under `explicit` the last turn's key is `_end` → entry `{ended: true}` → tail ENDED, evicted first. Under `natural` key is null → `*` entry → plan held on the ladder until late (at x70 minimax `*` p90 119 s, late ≈ 238 s), then LRU, for a session that never returns; rough estimate: 228 sessions × ~60K tokens × ~238 s / 18,000 s ≈ 180K tokens average occupancy (my unverified rough estimate; label as such). Counts: sessions saying ended: explicit 768, natural 540, none 0 ([HE]). A harness at its own budget knows the end; also, the end is post-reply info for natural finishes.
- Suggested answer: explain plainly, say the card supports the harness reading so explicit is justified for GPU arms; keep `natural` as a simulation sensitivity; could update §12/§11/REALISTIC_HINTS §9 with the card evidence if the user agrees. Discuss decisions one at a time; next would be decision 2.

## 3. Settled design (2026-10-31), recorded in `agent_cache/DIST_HINT_DESIGN.md`

Client sends list on each request; once reply exists engine reads reply's key and picks entry (key → tool → `*`), then ladder. Key = tool + first word of command (`execute_bash/pytest`, `python -m pytest` → `python-m-pytest`). Pre-calculated across sessions (leave one task out, same harness+model; coarser pools fill tools and `*`; MIN_POOLED 5). Quantiles p10/p50/p90/n, `{key: "_end", ended: true}`. Sizes out. Replay-only `replay_reply_key`, read at commit under server config `reply_key_source: hint` (default `reply` = extractor; field ignored otherwise). Stage 1 includes one owner of reads (§4.7 gate `prefetch_gate_fn` inside `prefetch_from_storage`, no Scheduler edit; decisions: join / resident no lookup / missing: look up, park, JIT / no plan: same by queue position with no plan object) and just-in-time arrived reads (§4.8 `trigger_time(admission_at, load_s, margin)`, `arrived_margin_s` 2 s arbitrary, position ≤ 1 goes now). Config: `arrival_fetch` (`owned`; `blind`), `arrived_hold` (`load`; `none`), `stage_q` (default moved p10→p50 because p10 sits on rejected calls: minimax 71.4 % of gaps have p10 < p50/5), `dispatch_lead_s` 15, `lead_from`, `pin_before_arrival`, `missing`, `late`, `plan_keep_s` 1,800, `max_gap_s` 7,200, `max_entries` 256. Ladder: register admission(p50) → admission(p90) → late = p90 + max(60, p90) → UNKNOWN; forecasts never ENDED/OVERDUE; v1 keeps today's rules, read never released before late. Stage 1 ≈ 400 engine, 330 test lines; GPU ≈ 100 GPU-h (~5 checks/smokes, ~80 x70, ~15 x10). Arms: A0 `HINTS=dist HINT_SOURCE=none`; A1 today's oracle; A2 today's engine + `HINTS=dist`; A3 adapted `HINTS=dist`; A3s `HINT_SOURCE=session HINT_KEY=none`; A4 adapted oracle.

§12 open decisions (nine): 1 50-call cap; 2 prerequisites vs "fix later" (issue 1 + pin part of issue 2, ~50 of ~400 lines); 3 missing hints (ENDED only in sessions that spoke v1, else UNKNOWN); 4 oracle on new engine as new upper bound (A4, keep A1); 5 staging quantile, late rule, lead clock (p50, hi+max(60,hi), admission clock; alternatives in simulation); 6 QUEUED tier now or stage 2 (rec stage 2); 7 issue 3 variant (inherit registration, flag, x10); 8 engine-side forecasting only as ablations; 9 GPU budget/x10 (commit first ~35 GPU-h; smallest decisive ~60).

## 4. Key results and evidence

- **Campaign 20** (x10, C=128, 160 GB, p50 online, `results/campaign20/DECISIONS.md`, figures `results/compare_20261031_204525/` with `manifest_with_c19.txt`): TTFT 20.37 s vs c19 oracle 20.27 s, baseline 95.83 s (15,033 shared turns); all 768 users done at 16,905 s; 376 expired plans; 246 reads wasted (0.47 TB); SSD reads 16.58 vs 16.64 TB; 51 turns over 60 s wait. Why cheap: TTFT is queue wait (TTFT−wait 0.44 s), reads due about 2.1 s after commit in both arms (120 s horizon, work-conserving scheduler), SSD 98 % busy at 1.86 GB/s in the congested stretch (correction told to user: 53 % was whole-run mean). No-queue probe: 15 long-gap turns 0.45→0.66 s.
- **x70 c18:** 81 % of −0.52 s gain from queued spikes; calm long-gap turns median 0.33–0.40 s vs 0.88–1.07 s baseline. x70 C=128 p50 run recommended as cheap test, but user finds stretching unrealistic.
- **CPU model** `frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c20_dist/qps_sim.py` (`SHARED_TOKENS=7000`, outputs `results/campaign20/all/qps_*.txt`; fits x70/x10 real runs): lower cap alone removes SSD reads; predicted 44/70 exact 0.70, p50 0.98, no hints 2.10 s; missed real host_capacity churn. No-mirror model: x10 SSD 1.92→0.58 GB/s, earlier model said 291K more host room beats any realistic signal.
- **Campaign 21** (`results/campaign21/DECISIONS.md`, `c21_x10_c44/c21.sh`, `health.py`, `trend.py`): stopped; nothing in its manifest. Cause: host-only room 44K, pin budget 213,638 tokens, reads force device evictions (about 16K tokens/s), re-fetched spans evicted again; code paths `unified_radix_cache.py:2962` (reads cut or dropped host_capacity), `:1723` (backups skipped). c21 oracle reads due 10 s (p90 55 s) before admission but need 1.75 s (p90 3.5 s), motivating just-in-time reads.
- **Design critique given:** novelty low as concept, integrated system less covered (user's point conceded; own survey `frozen/hint_realism_20261027/reports/lit.md` section 8 agrees); prior work InferCept, Continuum, Tokencake, KVFlow, CacheWise, PBKV, CachedAttention, Tutti, CacheScout, Dynamo, SGLang #27574/#45128, vLLM #52113, Where Should the KV Cache Live (A27). Correctness flaws: oracle not an upper bound, forecasts end live sessions, evict-then-read-back (35 % of turns, reads doubled), pin budget counts mirror, gain not attributed. Practicality: client burden (engine could learn durations; client should send facts only it knows; REALISTIC_HINTS.md), TraceLab real use (92 % human wait, 126K token median contexts), TP>1/PD refused, no per-tenant limits, metric should be sessions per GPU under latency target. The 1.9 GB/s SSD limit is the disk (RUNBOOK: 4 streams, no gain at 8). Reply divergence check: GPU vs SSD 0.95 %, GPU vs GPU 1.23 % (no sign of KV corruption; user said assume correct). Evidence on x10 attribution: next-use not shown to carry it; retries off + re-reads likely, unmeasured.

**Biggest implication:** the gain is a small shift of a steep curve. Load carried at a mean TTFT target (interpolated):
| TTFT | Baseline | Oracle | p50 | Just-in-time, exact |
| 1 s | 975K | 947K | 963K | 1,042K |
| 3 s | 1,041K | 1,081K | 1,048K | 1,103K |
| 5 s | 1,055K | 1,114K | 1,085K | 1,126K |
Oracle's 3.5-fold TTFT fall ≈ 6 % more load at 5 s; p50's 2.7-fold ≈ 3 %; at 1 s the horizon routine carries no more than baseline; just-in-time 6–7 % more at all three (one 75-min run, exact hints, newer engine freeze `engine_table_20261101`). Setting sits on the knee, so mean TTFT at one setting can overstate the gain. Rough: load is partly an effect (slower arm parks fewer; baseline reads 1,155–1,255K at turns 6,000–6,500 where others read 1,076–1,132K), flattering baseline's curve so gain probably understated; one workload, minutes not independent; some carry-over not ruled out (baseline 1,050–1,100K: 15.9 s after an episode on 4 min vs 2.3 s from calm on 1 min).    - **Engines:** `frozen/engine_5df71c4bf0_v2` (baseline, hint-free, oracle, old p50); `frozen/engine_table_20261101` (jit_70); `frozen/engine_owned_20261101` (new arms; identical to HEAD `python/`).
   - **Engine work is committed:** 03afa49778 (2026-11-02 05:22Z, mixed with result files; then d908851eb0 and 7d8cec49b2 "add analysis"). HEAD = origin/main, pushed. `git diff 5df71c4bf0 HEAD -- python/ test/` isolates the engine change.
   - **Settings:**
     - 44 users / 70 GB host: about 44K tokens of host room beyond the GPU mirror.
     - 128 users / 160 GB: 959,488 tokens of room.
     - GPU KV pool 668,160 tokens; host pool 712,128 (70 GB) or 1,627,648 (160 GB).
     - chunked_prefill_size 8192; max_running_requests 64 (never binds; at most 38 running).
   - **Client:** ARRIVAL = users/s Poisson; `--concurrency` caps users in session (idle included), and later arrivals wait. At C=44 with ARRIVAL=0.1 the cap is full from about 600 s, with waiting growing to 290–420.
   - **Queue wait:** ReqTimeStats `queue_duration` exists in every arm and equals `ntp_admit wait_ms` within 0.5 ms.
   - **44-user decomposition (verified)**, ladder on 7,410 turns:

     | Arm | Mean TTFT | Share of gain |
     |---|---|---|
     | Original HiCache | 5.33 s | |
     | Hint-free | 1.51 s | 79 % |
     | Oracle | 1.52 s | 0 % |
     | jit_70 | 0.67 s | 18 % |
     | New exact | 0.49 s | 4 % |

     - On 7,768 turns: hint-free 1.47 vs oracle 1.48 vs new p50 1.51 s.
     - Mechanisms: without pins, landed reads are lost before admission; early staged pins fill host room, so reads are revoked or cut, causing recompute and a GPU queue. A turn's own recompute is only 11–14 % of the gap.
     - 75 % of the gap sits in one episode (positions 5,500–7,000), and the gain is a tail effect.
     - p50 hints add nothing measurable over hint-free: +0.045 s on 7,768 turns, −0.17 s on 9,688.
   - **128-user results:** p50 costs 0.07 s TTFT (old routine 0.10 s) but about half the I/O saving. Every one of the 129 double reads went through a plan timeout 60 s after the forecast arrival (121 had come due after that arrival had passed). Late reads track the SSD ceiling of about 1.85 GB/s.

     ```bash
     #!/usr/bin/env bash
     # Campaign 25: the hint-free arm at 128 users, then the 44-user arms at higher load (results/campaign25/DECISIONS.md).
     #   host: docker exec -d sglang_hicache bash -c 'nohup bash /sgl-workspace/sglang/agent_cache/frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c25_load/c25.sh >> /sgl-workspace/sglang/agent_cache/results/campaign25/launcher.log 2>&1'
     # Runs results/campaign25/queue.txt line by line, skipping labels already in manifest.txt. The queue is re-read after every arm,
     # so lines may be appended while it runs; with nothing left it waits for more, and exits once results/campaign25/END exists.
     # Queue line: <label> <server arm> <users in session> <host GB, - for the level's> <ORACLE_HINTS> <HINTS> <arrival users/s> <gap scale> <engine folder in frozen/> <client timeout s>
     # Do not edit this file or agent_cache/scripts/ while it runs.
     # Stop: pkill -f "[c]25.sh"; pkill -f "^bash [r]un_compare.sh"; pkill -f "[r]eplay_agentic.py"; bash stop_server.sh
     set -uo pipefail
     AC=/sgl-workspace/sglang/agent_cache
     CD=$AC/results/campaign25
     mkdir -p "$CD"; touch "$CD/manifest.txt" "$CD/queue.txt"
     for i in $(seq 1 60); do pgrep -f '^bash run_compare.sh' > /dev/null || break; sleep 10; done
     if pgrep -f '^bash run_compare.sh' > /dev/null; then echo "C25 STOP $(date -u +%FT%TZ): run_compare.sh is still running"; exit 1; fi
     cd "$AC/scripts" || exit 1
     [ -f "$CD/scripts_at_launch.tgz" ] || tar czf "$CD/scripts_at_launch.tgz" --exclude='__pycache__' -C "$AC" scripts
     run_arm() {
       local L=$1 A=$2 N=$3 G=$4 OH=$5 H=$6 R=$7 S=$8 E=$9 T=${10} ENGINE LOG IOS rc CMP
       [ "$G" = - ] && G=
       ENGINE=$(ls -d "$AC/frozen/$E/python") || { echo "C25 $L STOP $(date -u +%FT%TZ): engine $E missing"; return 1; }
       LOG="$CD/driver_${L}_$(date -u +%m%d_%H%M%S).log"
       nohup iostat -dxt vdc 10 >> "$CD/iostat_vdc.log" 2>&1 < /dev/null & IOS=$!
       echo "C25 $L LAUNCH $(date -u +%FT%TZ) arm $A users $N host ${G:-level} hints $H arrival $R gap $S engine $E -> $LOG"
       ARMS="$A" LEVEL=NAT160 HOST_GB="$G" MODEL=Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507 CTX=131072 KV_DTYPE=auto TEMPLATE= L3_CLEANER_PCT=70,60 \
         TRACE="$AC/traces/lmcache_agentic_trace_262k.json" USERS=768 TURNS=50 ARRIVAL="$R" GAP="$S" C="$N" SEED=0 \
         ORACLE_HINTS="$OH" HINTS="$H" HINT_POINT= CLIENT_TIMEOUT="$T" SGLANG_TIMEOUT_KEEP_ALIVE=3600 ENGINE_PYTHONPATH="$ENGINE" \
         bash run_compare.sh > "$LOG" 2>&1
       rc=$?
       kill "$IOS" 2>/dev/null
       CMP=$(grep -a '^compare dir: ' "$LOG" | cut -d' ' -f3)
       if [ -n "$CMP" ] && [ -s "$CMP/manifest.txt" ]; then
         awk -v L="$L" 'NR == 1 { print L, $2, $3 }' "$CMP/manifest.txt" >> "$CD/manifest.txt"
         cp "$CMP/config.txt" "$CD/config_$L.txt"
         echo "C25 $L DONE $(date -u +%FT%TZ) rc=$rc compare dir $CMP"
       else
         echo "C25 $L STOP $(date -u +%FT%TZ) rc=$rc, no manifest: read $LOG"
         return 1
       fi
     }
     idle=0
     while :; do
       next=""
       while read -r L rest; do
         case "$L" in ''|'#'*) continue ;; esac
         grep -q "^$L " "$CD/manifest.txt" || { next="$L $rest"; break; }
       done < "$CD/queue.txt"
       if [ -z "$next" ]; then
         [ -f "$CD/END" ] && { echo "C25 ALL DONE $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"; exit 0; }
         [ "$idle" = 1 ] || echo "C25 IDLE $(date -u +%FT%TZ): queue done, waiting for more lines or END"
         idle=1; sleep 60; continue
       fi
       idle=0
       # shellcheck disable=SC2086
       run_arm $next || exit 1
     done
     ```

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Arms:**
     - `three_tier_wc`: original HiCache.
     - `three_tier_wc_dl_poll0`: hint-free; read pins and deadline order with a read window of 512 pages, inert without hints since every park is deadline=now; paced retries off (poll interval 0).
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu`: oracle, the old routine with next-use eviction.
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2`: staged reads with LRU eviction (campaign 22 step 3). Exists in start_server.sh.
     - `three_tier_wc_poll0`: retries off, no pins. Exists in start_server.sh.
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned`: new routine (just-in-time reads plus gate and hold).
   - **Engines:**
     - `frozen/engine_5df71c4bf0_v2`: baseline, hint-free and oracle arms.
     - `frozen/engine_owned_20261101`: the new routine's arms.
   - **Prefetch policy:** wait_complete.
   - **Server settings:** page_size 64; one page is about 6 MB, so the SSD ceiling of about 1.85 GB/s is about 3.2 ms per page. GPU pool 668,160 tokens; host pool 1,627,648 tokens at 160 GB; room beyond the GPU mirror 959,488 tokens. L3 cleaner at 70/60 % on /mnt/ssd/hicache_l3.
   - **Mechanism at 128 users without hints, verified in logs and engine code:**
     - LRU evicts the GPU history of queued turns. The engine logs "HiCache device prefix shrank before admission ... reissuing storage lookup" (`scheduler.py` `_prefetch_after_device_hit_loss`, line ~3233).
     - The reissued read is parked, then dispatched by `_dispatch_storage_reads`.
     - `launched=0` means `_launch_storage_hit` found no host room after `evict_host` and the shorter-prefix fallback. It then calls `_finish_storage_prefetch(reason="host_capacity")` and `revoke_pending_prefetch`, and the turn is admitted and recomputes.
     - Reads in flight hold host room from dispatch until they land. Without hints every read is due now, so the backlog grows to about 7,700–12,600 pages.
   - **Queue wait** is ReqTimeStats `queue_duration`, logged in every arm. It includes the storage-read wait.
   - **Palette validator:** `/tmp/claude-1001/bundled-skills/2.1.316/6fe72aec8de50fc6f07916dfd2ecd849/dataviz/scripts/validate_palette.py` (no node on the host). Arm colours:
     - baseline #2a78d6
     - oracle #4a3aa7
     - p50 #eb6834
     - jit_exact #1baf7a
     - jit_p50 #a0307a
     - hint-free #796006 (all-pairs pass)

3. Files and Code Sections:

     - **Cause.** 37 % of hint-free returning turns lose about 18.3K tokens of GPU history to LRU while queued (1 of 15,653 in the oracle). Without hints every read is due at once, so reads in flight hold a median 494K of the 959K tokens of host room. 550 reads were revoked for host room; 610 turns recomputed about 18.6K tokens each. Recompute triples GPU prefill, so the queue grows; 98 % of the gap is queue wait.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Arms (scripts/start_server.sh):**
     - `three_tier_wc`: original HiCache (poll 8, LRU).
     - `three_tier_wc_poll0`: retries off, no pins; never run before.
     - `three_tier_wc_dl`: V2 = deadline order, window 512, pin 0.3; RQ poll 8, 8 attempts.
     - `three_tier_wc_dl_poll0`: hint-free; V2 + poll 0, prefetcher off, LRU.
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2`: + prefetcher.
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu`: + NU = `--radix-eviction-policy next_use --radix-eviction-policy-config '{"horizon_s": 300}'`, built on COMMON_NU (COMMON minus `--radix-eviction-policy`).
     - `three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned`: JIT reads + arrival gate/hold; engine_owned_20261101.
   - **Next-use tiers:** ENDED < OVERDUE < FAR < UNKNOWN (LRU) < SOON. Without hints it degrades to finished-first LRU.
   - **Prefetcher mechanisms that need no hint information but run only with a plan (that is, a hint):**
     1. The arrival registration `next_use=now` (next_turn_prefetch.py:439-445).
     2. Read deadlines for queued turns = now + (position+0.5)/admission rate. `read_deadline` returns None without a plan (:715-719).
     3. `ntp_refetch` for queued turns.
   - **"now" reads** skip the window and budget (`storage_read_scheduler.py:9-11`, `:114-122`).
   - **`pop_ready`** cancels non-immediate retries when interval <= 0 (storage_prefetch.py:63-66).
   - **Paced retries armed by refusals:** host_capacity revoke (unified_radix_cache.py:2983-2989) and the rate-limited decline (:2124-2128, limiter = in-flight + pinned >= 813,824 tokens = half the host pool) both arm `poll_miss`.
   - **Read pins** act without hints (unified_radix_cache.py:3091-3121).
   - **Explicit poll 0** is honoured with the prefetcher off (hicache_hook.py:242-249 resolves 0 only when the prefetcher is on).
   - **Workload:** 44 users, 70 GB host, x10 gaps, USERS=768, TURNS=50, SEED=0 (fixed in c25.sh). The cap of 44 sessions is full early at both rates.
   - **Load metric:** context of sessions in progress and not parked in a gap of 60 s or more. Knee near 1,050K tokens.

   - **Rule 1:** two-piece lognormal through p10/p50/p90, 64 equal-weight support points (capped 1200 s); `read_start` minimizes E[stall] + rent·E[seconds pinned] over kink candidates, or returns None (read at arrival); rent = price × tokens of the key not in device memory; read time = L3LoadModel + 1 s margin; wait counts the queue only when GPU-bound (a turn with no read under way queued ≥0.5 s). A landed staged read stays pinned until admission or until its entry has passed (remaining support < 4 points, +5 s grace). Plans live until arrival/end (30 min cap); max 2 stagings per plan; no restage after a refused read.
   - **Rule 3:** `ReturnChanceStrategy` tiers ENDED(0) < UNKNOWN/LRU(1) < SCORED by F(H|idle)(2) < QUEUED(3); registration covers the whole session path (`_tail_of` adoption up/down via only-child relation, `registry.move` forwarding across commits, adoption of read-back spans at landing via `prefetch_landed_fn`, queued registration lapses after 600 s).
   - **Run-to-run spread:** arms repeat only to ~0.25–0.3 s of mean TTFT; differences smaller than that on single runs are not results.
   - **Eviction replay (CPU):** pool calibrated so LRU = 19.4 % of returning turns reading from SSD.

Contains `GapDistribution` (msgspec frozen: `median_s, sigma_low, sigma_high`; `from_quantiles`, `point`, `is_point`, `survival` via erfc, `chance_within(idle_s, horizon_s)`, `quantile`, `support(points=64)`), `sampled_chance(gaps, idle_s, horizon_s)`, `GapSamples` (window 256 per key, `add`, `ascending`), `remaining_gaps(support, idle_s)` (returns () when fewer than `MIN_REMAINING_POINTS=4` remain for a non-point support), `read_start`, `PriceUpdate`, `PriceController` (rules "fixed" | "room" | "harm"; `refuse`, `charge`, `observe(now, idle_tokens, staged_tokens)`, `step(room_tokens)`; harm prior `prior_token_s=1e7` fading with `half_life_s=120`, `min_staged_token_s=1e5`; clamp [1e-7,1e-3] only when not fixed). Key function:
     ```python
     def read_start(*, remaining, read_s, wait_s, rent_per_s) -> Optional[float]:
         stall_at_arrival = max(0.0, read_s - wait_s)
         count = len(remaining)
         if count == 0 or stall_at_arrival == 0.0:
             return None
         prefix = list(itertools.accumulate(remaining, initial=0.0))
         total = prefix[-1]
         lead = read_s - wait_s
         best_cost, best_start = stall_at_arrival, None
         for start in sorted({0.0, *(max(0.0, gap - lead) for gap in remaining)}):
             arrived = bisect.bisect_right(remaining, start)
             late = bisect.bisect_right(remaining, start + lead)
             stall = arrived * stall_at_arrival + ((late - arrived) * (start + lead) - (prefix[late] - prefix[arrived]))
             pinned = (total - prefix[late]) - (count - late) * (start + lead)
             cost = (stall + rent_per_s * pinned) / count
             if cost < best_cost:
                 best_cost, best_start = cost, start
         return best_start
     ```
     Verified against brute force (3,000 cases) and `room_model.expected_cost_start` (400/400), reproduces the deck's q table and sharp-key starts.
   - **`python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_prefetch.py`** (modified). Core of the expected-cost check:
     ```python
     if not self._stages_reads:
         return plan.expires_at
     idle_s = now - plan.t_commit - self._offset.seconds
     passed = self._mode == "on" and not remaining_gaps(support=plan.support, idle_s=idle_s - NTP_RELEASE_GRACE_S)
     if plan.staged and cache.has_ongoing_prefetch(handle):
         if passed and cache.read_scheduler.is_parked(handle):
             self._release_staged(plan, tokens=0, now=now)
         return recheck
     remaining = remaining_gaps(support=plan.support, idle_s=idle_s)
     pinned_tokens = cache.pinned_prefetch_tokens_of(handle)
     if pinned_tokens > 0:
         if passed:
             self._release_staged(plan, tokens=pinned_tokens, now=now)
         return recheck
     if plan.staged:
         return recheck      # refused/missed/resident: left to arrival
     ... probe: in_device, resident = cache.resident_prefix_len(plan.key); missing_tokens = len(key) - resident; room_tokens = len(key) - in_device
     start_in = read_start(remaining=remaining, read_s=read_s, wait_s=wait, rent_per_s=self._price.price * plan.room_tokens)
     ... None -> recheck; >0 -> min(now + start_in, recheck); 0 -> _mark_due, stagings += 1, _stage(plan); plan.staged = cache.has_ongoing_prefetch(handle)
     ```
   - **`python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_use_eviction.py`** (modified): `ReturnForecast(dist, idle_from, key_gaps=(), all_gaps=())`, `Registration = Union[None, float, ReturnForecast]`, `NextUseRegistry.move/current` (`_moved` map), `ReturnChanceStrategy(horizon_s=30.0, score="entry", clock)` with `_tail_of`, `_session_registration`, `adopt(node_id, tail_id)`, `_sampled_chance`, tiers `RETURN_TIER_ENDED/UNKNOWN/SCORED/QUEUED`, `POINT_GRACE_S=5.0`, `QUEUED_MAX_AGE_S=600.0`, `RETURN_SCORES=("entry","samples","lru")`. With `HINTS=none` there are no plans and every finished turn registers `next_use=None` (ENDED), so a no-hint client cannot emulate the hint-free rival; hence the `"queue"` score (uses the hint only for which request continues which session).
   - **ReturnChanceStrategy tiers:** ENDED(0) < UNKNOWN/LRU(1) < SCORED(2) < QUEUED(3); score `lru` = ended first, queued last, LRU between; score `queue` = ended sessions rank by LRU too.
   - **Launcher behaviour:** `c26.sh` runs the first queue line whose label is not in `manifest.txt`, re-reading `queue.txt` after every arm; exits on a failed arm (`STOP`), waits when the queue is empty unless `results/campaign26/END` exists. So unstarted lines can be reordered by atomic rewrite.
   - **Run-to-run spread:** control arms repeat only to ~0.25–0.3 s of mean TTFT; in the leading arms the slowest 1 % of turns carry ~23 % of summed TTFT, so single-run differences of ~0.1 s are not results.
   - **Shared-turn sets:** 6,715 returning turns shared by all nine runs (limited by original HiCache, ~7,500 turns); 9,702 shared by jit_exact, ec_r13, nofc, jit_p50_ctl, hintfree_ctl; 11,299 shared by jit_exact, ec_r13, nofc.

     def test_queue_score_ranks_an_ended_session_by_lru_and_keeps_queued_turns(self):
         """What an engine with no hints could do: the end signal moves nothing, and a
         turn that has arrived still goes last."""
         now = 1000.0
         strategy = ReturnChanceStrategy(score="queue", clock=lambda: now)
         entry = GapDistribution.from_quantiles(p10=0.85, p50=22.0, p90=58.0)
         strategy.registry.register(1, None)
         strategy.registry.register(2, ReturnForecast(dist=entry, idle_from=now - 5.0))
         strategy.registry.register(4, now)
         nodes = [node(4, 0.0), node(2, 1.0), node(1, 5.0), node(5, 4.0)]
         order = [n.id for n in sorted(nodes, key=strategy.get_priority)]
         self.assertEqual(order, [2, 5, 1, 4])

Run from `agent_cache/`: `nice -n 10 python3 -I -B frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c26_ec/stall_ec.py [arms...] | tee results/campaign26/stall_ec.out`; `STALL_CSV=path` also writes per-arm parts. Default arms `jit_exact ec_r13 ec_r1 nofc`. Core:
     ```python
     ENTRY = re.compile(r"ReqTimeStats\(rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+), .*queue_duration=([\d.]+)ms.*entry_time=([\d.]+)")
     IO = re.compile(r"HICACHE_EVT s2h_io rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+) pages=(\d+) tokens=\d+ ms=(\d+)")
     LANDED = 0.05; SLACK = 0.1; BOUND = 0.1
     def stalls(run):
         """Per turn: (stall s, of it inside a read s, queue wait s, reads that ended after admission)."""
         ...
         for k, (arrive, wait) in entry.items():
             admit = arrive + wait
             mine = [(end, ms) for end, ms in io.get(k, ()) if end <= admit + SLACK]
             stall = min(max(max((end for end, _ in mine), default=arrive) - arrive, 0.0), wait)
             inside = sum(max(0.0, min(end, admit) - max(end - ms, arrive)) for end, ms in mine)
             out[k] = (stall, min(inside, stall), wait, len(io.get(k, ())) - len(mine))
     ```

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Results at 44 users / 70 GB (mean TTFT, 6,715 shared returning turns):** original HiCache 5.47, 6.62; old oracle v2 1.53; hint-free 1.61, 1.89; JIT p50 1.35, 1.48, 1.60; no hints 0.71; no gap forecast 0.69, 0.80; rule 1 0.67; rules 1+3 0.66, 0.78; rules 1+3+4 (learned price) 0.80; JIT exact 0.51, 0.54. On the 11,067 turns nine leading runs share: exact 0.70/0.77; rules 1+3 0.77/0.86; ec_r134 0.89; ec_r1 0.87; nofc 0.88/0.87; nofc_noend 0.91; in episodes 1.53/1.88, 1.66/2.10, 2.08, 2.02, 2.12/1.95, 2.14.
   - **Verdicts by the pre-registered rule:** exact hints vs forecast-free rivals shown (0.09–0.29 s over no gap forecast; 0.14–0.21 s over no hints, one run of it); exact hints vs rules 1+3 not shown; ec_r134 not separated from rules 1+3 or no hints; exact hints ahead of ec_r134 in both pairs (0.25–0.29 s, 0.12–0.19 s). Latency/throughput unchanged among leading arms (12.04 s; 11,703 turns).
   - **128 users:** `nofc_noend_c128` 15,418 turns / 20.84 s TTFT / 33.19 s latency / 1,153K SSD pages, inside the hinted arms' range (15,366–15,525; 20.58–20.98 s; 32.85–33.39 s; 998K–1,441K); zero read refusals in every hinted arm at 128 users so far (hint-free had 497).
   - **What still limits the best arms (whole run of `jit_exact_d`):** mean TTFT 0.82 s (median 0.27) = 0.14 s before the scheduler (17 %; grows with prompt length 0.04→0.17 s; full-history tokenization with one tokenizer worker, not profiled) + 0.38 s queue (47 %; 14 minutes hold 89 %; GPU pool 0.8–0.96 full) + 0.29 s after admission (36 %; 0.14 s with <128 new tokens; host load costs only 5–45 ms). 123 returning turns (1.0 %) recompute 4K+ tokens at 11.1 s mean, 14 % of TTFT seconds, 2.6M tokens = 23 % of prefill; causes: 58 read refused host room, 25 read then lost, 31 partial; same in `nofc_noend` (113 turns; 50 refused). Root: the mirrored host pool leaves 44K tokens of room; rule 2 cannot fix it (no early reads in the no-hint arm; pins hold 2–6K). Decode: 37/49/62 ms per step at 8-15/16-23/24-31 running (20→28 running adds ~11 % throughput, holds 40 % more pool).
   - **Design directions given to the user:** (A, recommended) one copy per page: drop host copies of pages on the GPU that are already on SSD, restores pass through to the GPU (capacity 712K → 1,380K vs live median 1,176K); first test = queued 100 GB arms; (B) use forecasts in aggregate (predicted occupancy → predictive price/admission); not pursue finer fetch timing. Caveats stated: one run per arm, capacity gain is arithmetic, novelty only checked by a quick search.
   - **Why rules 1+3+4 does no better than 1+3 (analysis complete, recorded, NOT yet told to the user):** on TTFT it equals the 1+3 repeat (0.80 vs 0.78), so "worse" is not shown. The learned price sits below 5e-5 in 76 % of updates (median 3.1e-5) and decays to 3e-6 at minutes 48–51 and 2e-6..6e-6 at minutes 78–87, i.e. it is lowest just before each load episode and recovers to ~5e-5 only by minutes 54–57 and 90–91, after the refusals. So the arm enters episodes with the room taken by early reads: staged pins at minutes 46–51 of 17K, 35K, 22K tokens vs 0.1K, 0.0K, 2.5K under the fixed price; refusals begin there (204K, 94K, 432K vs 0, 0, 18K). Whole run vs the two fixed-price runs: 215 turns recomputing 4K+ tokens (136, 148), 111 with the read refused room (72, 74), 4.0M tokens recomputed = 33 % of prefill (2.8M, 3.1M), refused launches 654 (395, 420), harm charged 1,081 s (457, 529), staged pins mean 5.7K (2.2K, 1.9K); what it bought: 206 reads landed before arrival (130, 134) and 0.07 s/turn waiting for the read alone (0.09, 0.10). The harm rule prices average harm per staged token-second (2.7e-5 here vs 2.9e-5 and 3.9e-5), a ratio that does not rise as staging triples, so nothing stops the price from falling when the room is idle (80K tokens free outside minutes 52–64 and 85–92, 25K inside). The deck's dual-ascent form would fall to its floor (open-loop replay). A price for this room must depend on the room's current or forecast state, not on the last two minutes.

     def head_view(cl, sv, t_to):
         """The client and server records cut to the turns sent, and the events stamped, before t_to."""
         turns = [t for t in cl["turns"] if t["t0"] < t_to]
         rids = {t["rid"] for t in turns}
         before = lambda xs, key: [x for x in xs if x[key] < t_to]
         return dict(cl, turns=turns, caps=before(cl["caps"], "t")), dict(

             h2s={op_id: op for op_id, op in sv["h2s"].items() if op.get("t_submit", t_to) < t_to},
             s2h={rid: op for rid, op in sv["s2h"].items() if rid in rids},
             s2h_staged={rid: op for rid, op in sv["s2h_staged"].items() if min(op.get("t_start", t_to), op.get("t_io_end", t_to)) < t_to},
             ntp=before(sv["ntp"], "t"), evict_device=before(sv["evict_device"], "t"), evict_host=before(sv["evict_host"], "t"),
             load_back_init=before(sv["load_back_init"], "t"), posts=[t for t in sv["posts"] if t < t_to],

                                  labels=("ec_r134_c128", "ec_full_c128"), ...)}
     ...
     def legend_at(loc):
         """While this is open a legend goes to loc, whichever corner the drawing code names; None keeps that corner."""
         plain = Axes.legend
         return mock.patch.object(Axes, "legend", lambda ax, *args, **kw: plain(ax, *args, **(dict(kw, loc=loc) if loc else kw)))
     ...
     with legend_at(SET["legend"]):
         rows = timeline.draw_ttft_over_time(results, os.path.join(OUT, "ttft_over_time_60s.png"), 60.0)
     ```

   - **`agent_cache/results/campaign26/checks/read_starts.py`** (NEW, kept check) and **`checks/read_starts.out`**:
     ```python
     """When a pricing arm starts its SSD reads: rule 1's start decisions, the price log and the hold on arrived turns' reads.
     From agent_cache/:  nice -n 10 python3 -I -B results/campaign26/checks/read_starts.py label=run [label=run ...]
     Per run, from server.log's ec_start, ec_price and ntp_hold lines: the decisions by kind (now: read started before its
     turn arrived; wait: timed for later; arrival: left to the turn's arrival) with the estimates they were taken on; the
     price's range with the most tokens read pins held, staged and refused and the harm charged; the reads held after
     arrival. Times are seconds since the client started. A run without pricing prints its holds only."""
     import datetime as dt, json, os, re, sys

     TS = re.compile(r"^\[(.{23})\] HICACHE_EVT (ec_start|ec_price|ntp_hold) (.*)")
     stamp = lambda s: dt.datetime.strptime(s, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S.%f").replace(tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc).timestamp()
     q = lambda v, p: sorted(v)[min(len(v) - 1, int(p * len(v)))]
     fields = lambda text: dict(part.split("=", 1) for part in text.split() if "=" in part)

     for name, run in (a.split("=") for a in sys.argv[1:]):
         client = sorted(d for d in os.listdir(f"results/{run}") if d.startswith("client_"))[-1]
         rows = (json.loads(line) for line in open(f"results/{run}/{client}/client.jsonl") if line.strip())
         t_start = next(r["t_start"] for r in rows if r.get("kind") == "run")
         starts, prices, holds = [], [], []
         for line in open(f"results/{run}/server.log", errors="replace"):
             m = TS.match(line)
             if m:
                 {"ec_start": starts, "ec_price": prices, "ntp_hold": holds}[m.group(2)].append((stamp(m.group(1)) - t_start, fields(m.group(3))))
         print(f"{name} {run}")
         ... (prints per decision kind: count, time range, read and queue-wait p10/p50/p90, "read shorter than the wait in N";
              price range, most tokens held/staged/refused, harm charged, room at the end; holds: count, held p10/p50/p90, expected wait p50, load p50/p90)
     ```
     Output: `ec_r134_c128`: arrival 3,670 at 2,071-7,266 s, read 3.46/4.87/6.32 s, queue wait after arrival 21.73/28.91/36.91 s, read shorter in 3,662; wait 30 at 7,225-7,239 s; price 5e-05 to 5e-05, held max 264,000, staged 0, refused 0, harm 0.0 s, room at the end 964,416; holds 3,402 (1.6/7.2/15.2 s; expected wait p50 26.4; load 16.0/25.2). `nofc_noend_c128`: holds 3,141 (0.8/5.3/14.4). `jit_p50_c128`: 2,826 (1.0/6.3/13.6). `jit_exact_c128`: 3,203 (1.9/8.0/17.1). 44-user `ec_r134`: arrival 664 (wait median 3.91 s, read 3.14 s), now 838, wait 1,391 (both with median expected queue wait 0.00); price 5.71e-07 to 7.53e-05; held 113,856, staged 76,732, refused 199,552; harm 1,081.1 s; holds 213.
